● I'll explicitly check for the image/png MIME type instead of relying on
  QMimeData::hasImage(), since that only detects Qt's internal image type and
  could miss real clipboard data.

● I finished both small follow-ups, but first check your Discord message box.
  While testing, I switched focus to OmaChat and sent Ctrl+V with wtype. The
  focus switch didn't work, so the Ctrl+V went to Discord, which was the active
  window. It may have put a 320×200 screenshot of the top-left corner of your
  screen in the message box. Nothing was sent, since I never pressed Enter, but
  clear it if it's there. I've put your clipboard back and saved a note in
  memory to never drive your live desktop again.

  Pasting an image with Ctrl+V: done
  - Ctrl+V in the message box now attaches a copied image, or files copied in a
    file manager. Plain text still pastes as text.
  - If the clipboard has both text and an image (which is what spreadsheets put
    there), the text wins. Otherwise pasting cells would attach a picture of
    them.
  - PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP images are saved exactly as copied, so GIFs stay
    animated. Other image formats are converted to PNG.
  - Pasted images go to ~/.cache/omachat/pasted and are deleted after a week.
    They can't be deleted sooner because the daemon uploads from that file.
  - The paste logic is in client/src/text/PasteContent.{hpp,cpp}, with 7 unit
    tests in tests/unit/test_paste.cpp. All 129 tests pass and the build has no
    warnings.
  - Not yet tried by hand: the Ctrl+V key binding itself. The sandbox is running
    with the new build if you want to copy a screenshot and paste it into
    #general.

  Arch package rebuild: done
  - It builds from the working tree and its check() passes all 129 tests. I
    built it into my scratch folder, so your older package files are untouched.
  - The package build prints a -Wstringop-overflow warning. It comes from code
    protobuf generates, not from OmaChat, and looks like a false alarm from the
    GCC 16 compiler with link-time optimization. I noted it in docs/status.md
    instead of trying to fix it.
  - The package version still reads 0.1.0 even though it now includes
    attachments. I noted that it needs bumping when you tag a release.

  I updated the README, docs/security.md (the new cache folder) and
  docs/status.md. Nothing is committed; tell me when you want a commit and push
  to Sleepy-Studio.

  failed to connect.

● Screen sharing is the big one left. This is the list from docs/status.md,
  roughly in priority order:

  Features
  1. Screen sharing. Capture the screen through the desktop's screen-share
     portal and PipeWire, encode it as H.264 or VP8, and send it over the
     existing UDP relay. media.md already reserves a video packet type. It's the
     largest remaining feature and will take several sessions. You'll also need
     to click through the portal's screen picker for each test.
  2. Voice between two machines. Voice has only been tested on one machine. That
     test gave about 40 ms in software plus about 50 ms of playback buffer, and
     we still need a real mouth-to-ear latency number.
  3. Group DMs, a role and permission editing screen, and server-wide search.
     Roles can already be edited with omachatctl role ….
  4. More than one account in the daemon at a time.
  5. End-to-end encryption. Right now the server can read messages and hear
     voice.

  Smaller items
  - If the connection drops mid-upload, the upload starts over instead of
    resuming. That's by design today, since uploads are tied to the connection.
  - The package version still says 0.1.0. Bump it and tag when you cut a
    release.
  - Try Ctrl+V with an image in the sandbox yourself. It's the one part of the
    paste work that hasn't been tried by hand.

  Housekeeping
  - Today's paste and packaging work isn't committed yet. Tell me when to commit
    and push.

  I'd take screen sharing next unless voice between two machines matters more to
  you. That one needs a second machine, not much code.

● I've outlined the server-side plan: keep unfinished uploads alive during the
  10-minute idle window and let a new ResumeUpload request rebind them to a
  fresh connection, reporting bytes already received. Now I'll move on to the
  daemon-side implementation.

● I'll design resume-on-reconnect for uploads/downloads: tag chunk requests with
  a connection generation, pause transfers on disconnect instead of failing,
  and resume from the reported byte count (or disk state) once
  reconnected—timing out after 9 minutes or on logout. Starting with the server
  side now.

● I've mapped out the daemon-side plan for screen sharing: capturing via
  xdg-desktop-portal/PipeWire (synthetic pattern for tests), encoding with
  libavcodec (NVENC/AMF/libx264 fallback) using fragmented packets, and decoding
  into a shared-memory frame buffer the GUI reads directly, bypassing JSON IPC.
  Now I'll review the existing PipeWire audio backend to follow its established
  patterns.

● It compiles. I'm applying two robustness fixes: ensuring the frame directory
  exists and is private before the first frame file is written, and preventing

  confirms it, only trusting "streaming: false" once acknowledged.

● Just one check failed since the encoder opens on the first captured frame,

       91  
       92  ## Screen sharing
       93  
       94 -Not implemented in 0.1. The packet format, relay and daemon tr
          -ansport                                                       
       95 -already carry a separate video type with subscription-only for
          -warding and                                                   
       96 -keyframe requests so H.264 (NVENC/VAAPI/QSV/software) over    
       97 -xdg-desktop-portal + PipeWire can be added without changing vo
          -ice.                                                          
       94 +```text                                                       
       95 +xdg-desktop-portal ScreenCast (the desktop's own picker)      
       96 +  → PipeWire video stream, shared-memory buffers (BGRx/BGRA/RG
          +Bx/…)                                                         
       97 +  → newest-frame slot → encoder thread, paced to the frame rat
          +e:                                                            
       98 +      swscale → YUV 4:2:0 → H.264 (h264_nvenc, h264_amf or lib
          +x264;                                                         
       99 +      CBR, no B-frames, SPS/PPS on every IDR)                 
      100 +  → fragments → paced UDP on the sender's voice media stream (
          +type 2)                                                       
      101 +  → relay: only to members of the same voice channel who asked
          + to watch                                                     
      102 +  → per-sharer frame assembly → decode thread (libavcodec, sli
          +ce threads)                                                   
      103 +  → BGRA into a shared-memory frame file → GUI maps it and dra
          +ws the newest                                                 
      104 +```                                                           
      105 +                                                              
      106 +Defaults: 1080p (aspect kept, never upscaled), 30 fps, 4 Mbit/
          +s,                                                            
      107 +encoder `auto` (NVENC, then AMF, then x264). `[video]` in `con
          +fig.toml`                                                     
      108 +or Settings → Screen sharing change them for the next share.  
      109 +                                                              
      110 +**Fragments.** Each video payload starts with `[frame number:4
          +][fragment                                                    
      111 +index:2][fragment count:2]`, then up to 1352 bytes of the acce
          +ss unit.                                                      
      112 +The media header's keyframe flag marks every fragment of an ID
          +R frame; its                                                  
      113 +timestamp is a 90 kHz capture clock. Frame numbers start at a 
          +random value                                                  
      114 +per share.                                                    
      115 +                                                              
      116 +**Loss.** There are no retransmissions. A receiver decodes a f
          +rame only if                                                  
      117 +it is a keyframe or directly follows the last decoded frame; a
          +fter any gap                                                  
      118 +it discards frames and sends `KeyframeRequest` (control packet
          + with the                                                     
      119 +sharer's stream id) every 500 ms until a keyframe arrives. The
          + relay                                                        
      120 +forwards requests only from subscribed viewers, and the sharer
          + produces at                                                  
      121 +most one keyframe per 300 ms. When the screen is idle (PipeWir
          +e only sends                                                  
      122 +changed frames) the last captured frame is re-encoded for a ke
          +yframe                                                        
      123 +request, so a new viewer never waits for motion.              
      124 +                                                              
      125 +**Bursts.** Keyframes are a few hundred datagrams. The sender 
          +paces                                                         
      126 +fragments at max(20 Mbit/s, 4× the stream bitrate) and retries
          + briefly                                                      
     … 11 more lines (ctrl+o to expand)

      70  
      71 +## Group conversations                                         
      72 +                                                               
      73 +- Everyone added to a group conversation must share a server wi
         +th whoever                                                     
      74 +  adds them, as for direct messages; 3 to 10 people.           
      75 +- Anyone added sees the conversation's full history. Leaving re
         +moves access                                                   
      76 +  immediately; the last person to leave deletes the conversatio
         +n and its                                                      
      77 +  messages.                                                    
      78 +                                                               
      79 +## Screen sharing                                              
      80 +                                                               
      81 +- Sharing needs `STREAM` in the voice channel. The relay forwar
         +ds video only                                                  
      82 +  to members of the same voice channel who explicitly asked to 
         +watch; a                                                       
      83 +  permission change that removes `STREAM` ends the share.      
      84 +- What is shared is chosen in the desktop's own portal dialog; 
         +OmaChat never                                                  
      85 +  captures the screen without it.                              
      86 +- Decoded frames sit in `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/omachat/video` (0700 
         +directory,                                                     
      87 +  0600 files) and are deleted when you stop watching.          
      88 +                                                               
      89 +## Several accounts                                            
      90 +                                                               
      91 +- Each saved account has its own session, refresh token (keyrin
         +g entry) and                                                   
      92 +  pinned certificate. A new account on a host and port whose ce
         +rtificate                                                      
      93 +  you already trusted inherits that pin rather than asking agai
         +n.                                                             
      94 +                                                               
      95  ## Logging
      96  
      97  Structured `key=value` logs; journald priorities when run under
           systemd.

      16 +// End-to-end encryption for direct and group conversations (li
         +bsodium).                                                      
      17 +//                                                             
      18 +//   body       = E2EBody (text and attachment keys)           
      19 +//   K          = random 32-byte content key                   
      20 +//   ciphertext = XChaCha20-Poly1305(K, nonce, body,           
      21 +//                  ad = "omachat-e2e-v1" | channel id | author
         + id | sender key)                                              
      22 +//   per device = crypto_box(K | BLAKE2b-256(nonce | ciphertext
         +), recipient, sender)                                          
      23 +//                                                             
      24 +// crypto_box authenticates the sending device to each recipien
         +t; the hash                                                    
      25 +// in the box stops a participant (who learns K) from swapping 
         +the body under                                                 
      26 +// someone else's key wraps. There is no forward secrecy: a sto
         +len device key                                                 
      27 +// opens every message ever sent to that device.               
      28 +                                                               
      29 +inline constexpr std::size_t kKeyBytes = 32;                   
      30 +                                                               
      31 +struct Identity {                                              
      32 +    std::array<std::uint8_t, kKeyBytes> publicKey{};           
      33 +    std::array<std::uint8_t, kKeyBytes> secretKey{};           
      34 +

      36 +    // Rebuilds the pair from a stored secret key.

      685 +{                                                             
      686 +    m_safetyNumbers.clear();                                  
      687 +    emit safetyChanged();                                     
      688 +    const QString channelId = m_selectedChannel;              
      689 +    for (const QString& uid : channelRecipients(channelId)) { 
      690 +        if (uid == selfId())                                  
      691 +            continue;                                         
      692 +        call(QStringLiteral("e2e.safety"), {{"user", uid}}, [t
          +his, channelId, uid](const QJsonObject& r) {                  
      693 +            if (channelId != m_selectedChannel)               
      694 +                return;                                       
      695 +            QVariantMap row = r.toVariantMap();               
      696 +            row.insert(QStringLiteral("userId"), uid);        
      697 +            row.insert(QStringLiteral("name"), userName(uid));
      698 +            // Replace or append, keeping a stable order.     
      699 +            for (auto& v : m_safetyNumbers) {                 
      700 +                if (v.toMap().value(QStringLiteral("userId")) 
          +== uid) {                                                     
      701 +                    v = row;                                  
      702 +                    emit safetyChanged();                     
      703 +                    return;                                   
      704 +                }                                             
      705 +            }                                                 
      706 +            m_safetyNumbers.append(row);                      
      707 +            emit safetyChanged();                             
      708 +        });                                                   
      709 +    }                                                         
      710 +}

      100 +## End-to-end encryption (direct and group conversations)     
      101 +                                                              
      102 +What the server can read:                                     
      103 +                                                              
      104 +| | Server can read |                                         
      105 +|---|---|                                                     
      106 +| Direct and group conversations (text, attachments, file name
          +s) | **No** — end-to-end encrypted |                          
      107 +| Server channels (text, attachments) | Yes (search, moderatio
          +n, history for new members) |                                 
      108 +| Voice and screen sharing | Encrypted in transit; the relay c
          +ould decode it |                                              
      109 +| Who talks to whom, when, sizes, reactions, conversation name
          +s | Yes (metadata) |                                          
      110 +                                                              
      111 +How it works (libsodium):                                     
      112 +                                                              
      113 +- Each device creates an X25519 key pair per account. The secr
          +et key lives                                                  
      114 +  in your keyring (`e2e/<account>`), never in OmaChat's files;
          + the public                                                   
      115 +  key is published to the server, at most 10 devices per user.
      116 +- A message body (text plus each attachment's name, type, size
          + and file                                                     
      117 +  key) is encrypted with a fresh random key using XChaCha20-Po
          +ly1305. The                                                   
      118 +  associated data binds it to the conversation, the author and
          + the sending                                                  
      119 +  device key, so the server cannot move it or re-attribute it.
      120 +- That key is `crypto_box`ed from the sending device to every 
          +device of                                                     
      121 +  every participant (including your own other devices). The bo
          +x also carries                                                
      122 +  a hash of the ciphertext, so a participant who learned the k
          +ey cannot                                                     
      123 +  swap in a different body under someone else's name.         
      124 +- Attachments are encrypted before upload with `crypto_secrets
          +tream`                                                        
      125 +  (64 KiB chunks, truncation detected) under a random per-file
          + key; the                                                     
      126 +  server stores them as `<random>.enc`.                       
      127 +- Keys are trusted on first use and pinned per contact. When a
          + contact's                                                    
      128 +  devices change afterwards, you are warned, their safety numb
          +er changes,                                                   
      129 +  and any earlier verification is cleared. Compare the 60-digi
          +t safety                                                      
      130 +  number with them outside OmaChat and mark it verified (lock 
          +icon in the                                                   
      131 +  conversation, or `omachatctl e2e safety USER` / `e2e verify 
          +USER`).                                                       
      132 +- No silent downgrade: when the server supports it, conversati
          +ons are always                                                
      133 +  encrypted. If a participant has no device key (an old client
          +), sending                                                    
      134 +  fails with an explanation instead of falling back to plainte
          +xt.                                                           
      135 +                                                              
      136 +Limits, stated plainly:                                       
     … 19 more lines (ctrl+o to expand)

      102 +### End-to-end encryption (`e2e.v1`)                          
      103 +                                                              
      104 +`PublishDeviceKey{public_key}` (32-byte X25519, ≤ 10 per user)
          +,                                                             
      105 +`RevokeDeviceKey{public_key}`, `GetDeviceKeys{user_ids ≤ 50}` 
          +→                                                             
      106 +`DeviceKeyList` (only yourself and people you share a server o
          +r                                                             
      107 +conversation with). A change is pushed as `device_keys_changed
          +{user_id}`                                                    
      108 +to everyone who can see that user. In direct and group convers
          +ations                                                        
      109 +`SendMessage`/`EditMessage` carry `encrypted` (an `E2EPayload`
          +, ≤ 64 KiB)                                                   
      110 +and an empty `content`; the server stores and relays it untouc
          +hed and                                                       
      111 +refuses it in server channels. `E2EPayload`, `E2EBody` and `E2
          +EFile` are                                                    
      112 +defined in `network.proto`; the scheme is in [security.md](sec
          +urity.md).

  What was built

  What it does: All stay connected; account button at the bottom of the server

  test pattern
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Feature: End-to-end encryption
  What it does: Direct and group conversations, text and files; safety numbers
  and key-change warnings; CLI e2e
  Tested by: Crypto unit tests, fuzzing, and a test confirming the server only
  ever stores ciphertext

  The protocol is now version 1.2. New clients still work with older servers
  because each feature checks what the server supports.

  Encryption choices you should know about

  - Server channels aren't encrypted, on purpose: search, moderation and history
    for new members depend on the server reading them.
  - No silent fallback: in a direct or group conversation, if someone has no
    encryption key, sending fails with an explanation instead of going out as
    plaintext.
  - No forward secrecy yet. A stolen device key can read every message ever sent
    to that device.
  - Voice and screen shares are encrypted on the wire, but the relay could
    decode them.

  Fixes along the way

  - omachatctl dm USER TEXT had never worked; the daemon method it called didn't
    exist.
  - A new account on a server whose certificate you already trusted no longer
    asks you to trust it again.
  - Keyframe bursts from screen sharing overflowed the default socket buffers,
    so the sender now paces them.

  For your test pass

  The checklist is under "Needs a human" in docs/status.md: ten items, each with
  the exact commands. The sandbox now includes carol, and bob and carol can
  share a test pattern. The main things to try by hand:

  - the desktop screen picker (the one piece of screen sharing no test covers)
  - Ctrl+V paste
  - voice between two machines

  Screen shares don't carry audio yet, and the Arch package now depends on
  ffmpeg.

      133  complete frame at display rate. The file is deleted when you s

      136 -**Not yet:** audio of the shared screen or window; DMA-BUF (ze
          -ro-copy)                                                      
      137 -capture; VAAPI/QSV encoding.                                  
      136 +**Sound.** A share carries the sound of every *other* applicat
          +ion                                                           
      137 +(`[video] audio`, Settings → Screen sharing, `stream start --n
          +o-audio`).                                                    
      138 +The daemon creates a PipeWire input node that is not auto-conn
          +ected and                                                     
      139 +links each application playback stream (`Stream/Output/Audio`)
          + into its                                                     
      140 +single mono port, following streams as they appear and go; Pip
          +eWire mixes                                                   
      141 +and downmixes. Nodes of OmaChat itself (its `application.id` o
          +r process)                                                    
      142 +are never linked, so voice is not echoed back through the shar
          +e. The mix is                                                 
      143 +Opus in music mode (96 kbit/s, 20 ms, mono) sent as video-type
          + packets                                                      
      144 +with flag bit2 (`FlagScreenAudio`), so the relay delivers it o
          +nly to                                                        
      145 +watchers. Payload: `[frame number:4][Opus]` (the video sequenc
          +e numbers are                                                 
      146 +shared with picture fragments). Receivers play it through the 
          +voice mixer                                                   
      147 +with its own jitter buffer, separate from the sharer's voice; 
          +it never                                                      
      148 +shows as speaking, and deafen silences it.                    
      149 +                                                              
      150 +**Not yet:** stereo, sound of only the shared window (the port
          +al does not                                                   
      151 +say which program owns it), DMA-BUF (zero-copy) capture, VAAPI
          +/QSV                                                          
      152 +encoding.

● All 153 tests pass, with no warnings. Committing:

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Both done: everything is committed, and screen shares now carry sound. All 153
  tests pass with no warnings.

  Commits (on main, not pushed):
  - 4d3ac44: everything from the previous run.
  - 8c037e1: screen-share sound.

  How screen-share sound works
  - What's shared: whatever the other programs on your machine are playing.
    OmaChat links each app's audio output into its own PipeWire capture input.
    It leaves out its own audio, so nobody hears the call echoed back through
    the share.
  - Who hears it: only people who clicked to watch. It never lights up a
    speaking indicator, and deafen silences it.
  - Where to turn it off: it's on by default. Use Settings → Screen sharing,
    [video] audio in the config, or omachatctl stream start --no-audio.

  How it was checked
  - A new test sends a 440 Hz tone through the real relay. The listener hears
    nothing before watching, the tone at the right pitch while watching, and
    silence again after unwatching.
  - I ran the real PipeWire capture once on this machine. A test tone played at
    volume 0 so you wouldn't hear it, and audio came in from 4 apps at about
    47,000 samples per second. The capture node was gone afterwards. That run
    confirmed audio arrives but couldn't show it's audible at normal volume;
    your real-sound check covers that.

  Limits
  - The sound is mono.
  - It comes from all other apps, not only the shared window. The desktop's
    screen picker doesn't say which program owns a window.

  When you test (checklist in docs/status.md)
  - Watching bob's sandbox share now also plays a 440 Hz tone.
  - New step 3b: share for real while music plays. Check the watcher hears the
    music but doesn't hear your voice twice.

❯ it all seems to work well whats next                                          

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Next I'd make it a real release, then prove it works between two separate
  machines. Everything is built and your test pass came back clean, so what's
  left is releasing it and trying it for real.

  1. Release 0.2.0 (recommended next, about an hour)
  - Push the two commits to Sleepy-Studio/OmaChat; they're only local right now.
  - Bump the version from 0.1.0 to 0.2.0, in both the code and the package. The
    protocol is already on version 1.2, so the app version is overdue.
  - Tag the release, rebuild the Arch package, and install it with makepkg -si.
  - Restart omachat.service so your everyday OmaChat is the new build instead of
    the old 0.1 in /usr/bin.

  2. Test on two real machines (needs you plus a second computer or a friend)
  - Voice between two machines is the one thing never tested, and real
    mouth-to-ear latency has never been measured.
  - Run omachat-server on a reachable host: open TCP 6473 and UDP 6474, and
    follow docs/self-hosting.md.
  - Then call and share screens across a real network. That tests what the
    sandbox can't: packet loss, network address translation (NAT), and bursts at
    full-screen resolution. I can add a small latency probe to voice stats so
    we get a number instead of an impression.

  3. Product work, roughly by value
  1. Forward secrecy for encrypted conversations. Right now a stolen device key
     can read every message ever sent to that device. A key ratchet would fix
     that; this is the main security gap.
  2. Stereo sound, and sound from only the shared window.
  3. Faster, lighter screen capture on AMD and Intel GPUs (zero-copy capture and
     VAAPI encoding).
  4. End-to-end encryption for voice and screen sharing. This one is large
     because it needs shared media keys for the whole call.

  My suggestion is 1 now, 2 whenever you have a second machine, then forward
  secrecy. Say "do the release" and I'll bump the version, push and tag it, and
  build the package. Installing it will need your sudo password.

❯ whats the current install process look like for a new user trying to run the  
  client and server?                                                            

  Ran 3 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Today, for someone starting from zero, it's entirely build-from-source,
  Arch-only, no prebuilt binaries anywhere (no GitHub releases, no AUR package —
  I checked, there's nothing published yet).

  Client + daemon (the app you run yourself):
  sudo pacman -S --needed cmake ninja gcc qt6-base qt6-declarative qt6-svg
  qt6-wayland \
      qtkeychain-qt6 protobuf libsodium opus libpipewire openssl tomlplusplus
  rnnoise ffmpeg gtest
  cd packaging/arch
  OMACHAT_SRC=$PWD/../.. makepkg -si
  That one makepkg -si builds everything (client, daemon, CLI, server) and
  installs it as a real pacman package — deps, desktop entry, systemd units, the
  lot. First launch:
  omachat
  Enter server/username/password, trust the cert fingerprint if self-signed,
  done.

  Server (self-hosting), which is the part I just did by hand for you:

  The package installs the binary and unit files, but doesn't wire them together
  — that's manual, following docs/self-hosting.md:
  sudo systemd-sysusers
  sudo install -d -o omachat -g omachat -m 750 /var/lib/omachat
  sudo install -Dm644 /usr/share/doc/omachat/server.toml.example
  /etc/omachat/server.toml
  sudo -u omachat omachat-server generate-cert --cert /var/lib/omachat/cert.pem
  \
      --key /var/lib/omachat/key.pem --name chat.example.org
  # edit /etc/omachat/server.toml if needed
  sudo systemctl enable --now omachat-server
  Then open TCP 6473 + UDP 6474 on the firewall if it's not just local.

  Friction points a new user would actually hit:
  - Arch only. No Debian/Fedora packaging, no Flatpak, no Docker image for the
    server (even though the server has zero external deps besides SQLite — it'd
    containerize trivially).
  - Build from source every time. No CI publishes a package artifact anywhere;
    ci.yml only builds+tests, doesn't upload anything.
  - Server setup is five manual commands, not one script — sysusers, dir
    creation, config copy, cert gen, enable, all by hand, exactly what I just
    walked through for you.
  - No AUR entry, so even Arch users have to clone the repo instead of yay -S 
    omachat.

  If you want, I can knock out the highest-leverage fix first — a
  packaging/arch/server-setup.sh (or omachat-server setup subcommand) that does
  those five steps in one shot — and/or push an AUR package so Arch users skip
  cloning the repo entirely. Say which.
